Juristic Platform · Documentation

Test Plan, Test Cases & E2E Automation

Owner: Senior QA EngineerDate: 2026-09-06Status: Draft v1.0Version: 1.0
Contents

1. Introduction

This document is the test plan for Juristic Platform, the web application that manages apartment water, electricity and common-area fees and notifies tenants through a LINE Official Account. It contains four things: the QA strategy that says what is tested at which tier and with which tool, 284 executable test cases, seven end-to-end browser scenarios, and the traceability matrix that proves every requirement and every product decision has at least one case behind it.

It is written against three binding sources and adds nothing to them: the PRD (scope, the §28 demo scenario, the §24 exclusions), the product decision log D-01 … D-47 (policy) and the engineering specification FR-01 … FR-48, NFR-01 … NFR-26 (rules, endpoints and error codes), with ADR-001 binding for stack and naming. Where this document introduces a name, a file or a hook of its own it is recorded in the assumptions block of the section that introduces it, and nowhere else.

1.1 Purpose, scope and audience

ReaderReadsTo answer
The developer about to open a pull request §2.7 entry criteria, then the cases in §3 – §6 tracing to the FR they touched "Which cases must be green before I push, and which ids do I put in my test titles?"
The tester running a milestone pass §3 – §6 top to bottom, then §2.7 exit criteria "What exactly do I type, and what exactly must appear?"
Whoever runs the demo §7.1 E2E-01, and the regression sets at the end of §3 and §4 "Does the graded path still work on this build, this morning?"
The reviewer or examiner §8, then any row that looks thin "Is anything in the specification untested?"
The PM and the two document owners The assumptions and contradiction callouts in each section "Which of my documents needs correcting, and what did QA assume in the meantime?"

Out of scope for this document. Load and concurrency benchmarking, a browser matrix beyond D-45's two targets, printed output (which no browser can assert), and the real LINE platform — the four things §2.1 deliberately leaves to a dated manual check. Nothing here tests a PRD §24 exclusion: there is no rent, no lease contract, no mobile app, no payment gateway, no card, no online banking, no slip upload, no QR payment, no IoT meter and no AI anywhere in this plan, and a case that would need one is a scope defect rather than a missing test.

1.2 How to read a test case

Sections 3 – 6 are one table per feature group. Every row is one executable case with nine columns, and every expected result states an exact value: an HTTP status, an UPPER_SNAKE error code, a Thai sentence quoted character for character from the design or the decision log, or an amount in the 1,790.00 form. A case that says "422 READING_BELOW_PREVIOUS" fails if the response is a 400, a 409, or a 422 carrying a different code.

ColumnWhat it holds
IDTC-AUTH-nnn, TC-ROOM-nnn, TC-BILL-nnn or TC-PAY-nnn. The prefix names the section, the number never moves once allocated, and the four sequences are independent so a group can grow without renumbering another.
TitleOne sentence naming the behaviour under test, not the steps.
TypeThe tier the case runs at — see below.
PriP0, P1 or P2 — see below.
PreconditionsThe state the case assumes on top of the standing fixture of §1.3. "Seed loaded" means exactly that fixture and nothing else.
StepsThe ordered actions, as a route and a control name or as an HTTP call.
Test dataThe literal input — a request body, a form value, a header.
Expected resultEverything that must be true afterwards: the response, the rendered Thai copy, and the database rows. A case that asserts only a status code is an incomplete case.
Traces toThe FR-xx the case discharges and the D-xx decisions it derives from. A case with no trace has no authority and does not appear.

Type — the tier the case runs at

unit — a pure function or one SQL expression, no database and no HTTP.
API — one request against a scratch schema, asserted on status, error code and payload.
UI — one rendered screen or print sheet, asserted on Thai copy, field state and test hooks.
e2e — a sequence crossing at least two screens or two roles, ending in a persisted effect.

The four map onto the tiers of §2.2: unit is tier 1, API and e2e are tiers 2 and 3, UI is asserted at tier 2 for rendered copy and at tier 4 where a browser is genuinely needed.

Priority — what a red case blocks

P0 demo-critical or money-touching. A red P0 blocks the defence: it can produce a wrong amount, a duplicate receipt, a leaked role boundary or a message a tenant should never have received. All P0s run before every push and on the morning of the defence.
P1 a documented rule a grader or the office will exercise, whose failure does not corrupt money. Fixed inside the milestone.
P2 boundary, copy and formatting detail. Batched.

Priority is about the case; §2.8's S1 – S4 is about the defect a red case reveals. They are related but not the same scale — a P2 case can find an S1 defect.

Two rules that decide whether a case is written correctly
  1. Assert the arithmetic, never the calendar (D-44). db/seed.sql computes every date from the run date, so a case that asserts 5 ก.ย. 2026 or the literal REC-202609-000852 goes red on the wrong day. Those values appear throughout this document as documentation values; the executable assertion is on due_date = MAX(due day, approval + 3 days), on REC-<payment month YYYYMM>-<6 digits>, and on 10 × 18 = 180 / 70 × 8 = 560 / 30 × 35 = 1,050 / total 1,790.00.
  2. Money format differs by surface (D-03). Tables, Bill Detail and the receipt render 1,790.00; LINE messages and sentence text render 1,790 บาท with the trailing .00 dropped. A case that asserts the wrong form on the wrong surface is a defective case, not a defect.

1.3 The standing fixture

Unless a case says otherwise, the database is rebuilt from db/schema.sql + db/seed.sql immediately before the case runs, the process runs with TZ=Asia/Bangkok, MySQL runs at time_zone='+07:00', and settings.line_test_mode = 1 so no message can reach a real phone (D-34, D-44). Section 2.4 describes the fixture as a QA artefact; the table below is the shortlist a case may depend on.

The seed is the finished period, not the demo's starting position

Room 205 arrives already read (812, 813) and already carrying its Draft bill 118. Any case that has to prove the reading step, or that generation creates a bill rather than recalculating one, must first apply test/fixtures/reset-205.sql (§2.4.1) — and every such case names it in its preconditions. On a demo or production database the equivalent is npm run db:demo-arm, which applies db/demo-reset.sql: the same unwinding without the test-recipient whitelist, which must never reach a demo database. The two files are separate for exactly that reason.

The cards above are the shortlist. §2.4 is the authoritative description of the fixture — every seed fact with the case that depends on it, the four scenario .sql files, and the three PRD §16 / §19 figures that are illustrative and must never be used as an expected value.

Editorial decisions taken while assembling this document
  1. Case ids are as their authors allocated them — TC-AUTH-001 … 072, TC-ROOM-001 … 072, TC-BILL-001 … 069, TC-PAY-001 … 071, 284 in total. The four prefixes are disjoint, so no id needed renumbering during the merge and every id in a section roll-up still resolves. The six ids above 069 were appended on 2026-09-07 for the two endpoints the engineering specification added (GET /payments/history, POST /rooms/:id/activate) and for the deployed-instance CSP smoke check of §2.3.1; nothing was renumbered.
  2. Room 205's tenant is สมชาย ใจดี, tenant id 37. The engineering specification's fixture table and its db/seed.sql excerpt say so, and that is the row a test meets. The UI design's cast table named นางสาวอารยา ศรีสุวรรณ instead; that document has since adopted the seed row, moving อารยา ศรีสุวรรณ to room 202, so the three documents now agree. No case asserts a literal tenant name either way. Closed as an S3 documentation defect (§9).
  3. The legend, the priorities and the standing fixture are stated once — in §1.2 and §1.3 — and each of §3 – §6 keeps only what is specific to it. Nothing was deleted that a case depends on.
  4. Section numbering is this document's, cross-document references are not. A bare §n in the prose points inside this document; a reference to another document always names it ("engineering specification §3.3.1", "ADR §6.9", "PRD §28").

2. Test Strategy

This section is the QA contract for Juristic Platform. It says what is tested, at which tier, with which tool, against which data, and what has to be true before a build is shown to anyone. Everything below is derived from ADR-001 (binding for stack and conventions), the PM decision log D-01…D-47 (binding for policy) and the engineering specification's FR-01…FR-48. Where this document adds a name, a hook or a file, it is listed in Assumptions recorded by this section and nowhere else.

Three sentences fix the shape of everything that follows. Money is computed in SQL on DECIMAL and never in JavaScript (D-16, ADR A-4), so an amount can only be asserted against a database or a rendered page — never against a JavaScript expression. Overdue is derived from CURDATE() and is never stored (D-21), so a due-date test moves the data, not the clock. Every seed date is computed from the run date (D-44), so a test asserts the arithmetic — 10, 70, 180.00, 560.00, 1,050.00, 1,790.00 — and never the calendar.

2.1 Objectives

O-1 · The number on the receipt is right

Every amount a tenant sees on LINE, on the printed ใบเสร็จรับเงิน or on Bill Detail equals the sum of its own printed lines, and every line equals ROUND(quantity × unit_price, 2) against the rate snapshotted on the bill. A total that disagrees with its lines is the defect this project exists to prevent (D-16, D-41).

O-2 · A double-click cannot lose money

Two cashiers, a double-submitted form and a retrying scheduler all converge on one payment, one receipt number and one LINE message. The guarantees live in thirteen unique keys, three stored generated columns and twenty-one CHECK constraints — validation layer 4, enumerated key by key in engineering specification §3.6. (ADR §6.8 says eleven; it predates uq_rooms(room_code) and uq_users(username), and the specification supersedes the figure.) So the tests that matter drive HTTP concurrently and assert what the database allowed, not what the button did.

O-3 · Staff never receive an amount

D-36 is stronger than navigation: money is absent from the queries that serve staff, not hidden in a template. Every Staff-forbidden route is asserted twice — HTTP 403 with the Thai page or FORBIDDEN_ROLE, and no digit-grouped amount anywhere in the response body.

O-4 · The graded demo path always runs

The PRD §28 walk — read → generate → approve → LINE → cash → receipt → LINE → Dashboard — is exercised end to end on every push at two tiers: headless in npm run rehearse and through a real browser in E2E-01. A red E2E-01 blocks the merge.

O-5 · Nothing from PRD §24 reappears

No test, fixture, seed row or page object mentions rent, a lease, a gateway, a card, online banking, a slip, a QR code, an IoT meter or AI. A test that would need one of them is a scope defect, not a missing feature (D-05).

O-6 · Failures are legible in Thai

Every rejection path a user can reach is asserted on both halves: the stable UPPER_SNAKE error code that tests read, and the Thai sentence the office reads (ADR §6.3, D-40). Asserting only the status code passes while the screen shows English.

Out of scope for QA, deliberately

  • Load and concurrency benchmarking. D-45 makes no uptime, concurrency or load commitment. The five committed numbers are stopwatch measurements against the seed, taken by hand, recorded in the test log.
  • A browser matrix. D-45 names current Chrome and Edge at ≥ 1280 px for Admin and Chrome for Android at 360 × 640 for Staff. Playwright runs Chromium only; Edge and a real Android handset are checked by hand once per milestone.
  • Print output. Browser print CSS is the one thing in this stack that cannot be automated (ADR A-13.5). The A5 ใบเสร็จรับเงิน is printed on the real office printer by week 9 — a dated manual test, not an assertion.
  • The real LINE platform. Automated tiers never call it. One real link and one real push are performed by hand per milestone against the deployed instance, because the free plan's 500-message allowance is the project's scarcest resource (D-34, R-02).

2.2 The test pyramid

flowchart TB
  U["Tier 1 · Unit · node:test · no database · about 40 tests · 1 second"]
  I["Tier 2 · API integration · node:test plus fetch · scratch schema · about 25 tests"]
  R["Tier 3 · Rehearsal · npm run rehearse · the demo path, no browser"]
  E["Tier 4 · Browser E2E · Playwright · 7 scenarios · the screens an examiner sees"]
  Q["Guarantee · the money is right and cannot be double-taken"]
  D["Guarantee · demo day survives a double-click and a LINE outage"]
  U --> I --> R --> E
  U -. arithmetic and formatting .-> Q
  I -. constraints, races and roles .-> Q
  R -. the path runs headless .-> D
  E -. the screens agree with the database .-> D
Four tiers, three of them free of any test dependency. Tiers 1–3 are ADR §6.9 verbatim; tier 4 is the one addition this test plan makes, granted as a scoped amendment to A-9 and costed at 2 person-days in M8 — and it is the first thing cut if the semester runs short.
TierWhat belongs hereTool — exactly as ADR §1 names itRunnerRuns
1 · Unit
no database		 Pure functions only: previous-reading resolution over row fixtures, unit computation including the meter-replacement branch, rate resolution by period, the MAX(due day, approval + 3) rule and its clamp, billStatus(), thaiBaht(), lib/money.js formatting, receipt-number formatting, the CSRF timingSafeEqual compare, template rendering with braced placeholders. node:test + node:assert/strict. Node 24 ships both. no Jest no Vitest npm run test:unit On save
2 · API integration
scratch schema		 The races that lose money and the rules that only exist in the database: double confirm, void then re-pay, generation twice, approve twice, the daily job twice, concurrent receipt allocation, the dedupe_key, revert with a later bill, and one test per Staff-forbidden route asserting 403 and that no amount appears in the body. node:test + built-in fetch + a 15-line cookie helper (test/helpers/client.js). no supertest npm run test:int Before every push, in CI
3 · Rehearsal
the demo path		 One script that walks the PRD §28 path headless against seed.sql with LINE test mode ON, asserts the arithmetic and never the calendar, and prints the six §28 numbers. test/rehearsal.js, plain Node, no framework. npm run rehearse Before every push and on the morning of the defence
4 · Browser E2E
added by this plan		 Seven scenarios that can only be proved through a rendered page: the confirmation dialogs of D-39, the phone reading round at 360 px, the Thai 403 page, the sticky counter, the derived Overdue badge, the drain loop on the approval result screen, and the receipt as an examiner sees it. @playwright/test (TypeScript), Chromium only, devDependencies. Granted as an A-9 amendment — see below. npm run test:e2e In CI on main and every pull request
ADR-001 A-9 amendment — Playwright is tier 4, dev-only. granted

Status: granted. The amendment requested below was accepted by the architecture judge and is written into the engineering specification — the stack table's Tests row, the rejected-dependency paragraph, A-9's own row, the repository tree (§6.1.1), .env.example (§6.2.3), the npm scripts test:e2e and trace (§6.2.4), the fourth tier (§6.5.1) and 2 person-days added to M8 (§6.4.1, §6.4.3). Two terms came back with it and are binding on this document: tier 4 is first in the cut order, ahead of every A-14 item, and it stays Chromium-only, workers: 1, retries: 0, seven specs. §9.3 #9 is closed.

The request, kept for the record:

  • What is added: one devDependency, one playwright.config.ts, one test/e2e/ folder and one CI job. Nothing reaches the runtime, package.json's ten runtime dependencies are untouched, and the deployed process is byte-identical.
  • TypeScript does not reintroduce a build step. A-1 removes the build step for the application; Playwright compiles its own specs, so test/e2e/*.spec.ts needs a nine-line tsconfig.json scoped to that folder and no toolchain the app can see. npm start, npm run dev and the Render build command are unchanged.
  • Why it earns its place: four of the seven scenarios assert something no headless tier can reach — a D-39 dialog that must state the exact effect before an irreversible action, the reading round on a 360 px viewport (D-45 target 1), the Thai 403 page (D-40), and the derived เกินกำหนด badge. The rehearsal script proves the path runs; only a browser proves the screens agree with the database.
  • Cost containment: Chromium only, workers: 1, retries: 0, seven specs. Setup is npx playwright install --with-deps chromium in one CI job — measured at roughly two hours of work, not a week, because the seed already rebuilds the whole demo in one command (D-44).
  • Cut order: if the semester runs short, tier 4 is cut before item (1) of A-14, because it ships no screen. Tier 3 is the safety net that survives the cut, and it is the tier A-14 already protects.

Had the amendment been declined, every scenario would have stayed valid as a written manual test and the Playwright code in §7 would have been deleted; nothing else in this document would have changed. That branch is now closed — the code in §7.8 ships.

What is deliberately not a tier

  • No coverage gate. ADR §6.9 is explicit and QA agrees: the enforced artefact is the list of tests, not a percentage. A missing test from the tier-2 list is a gap; a coverage number is a number. The traceability matrix in §2.9 replaces it.
  • No snapshot testing. Every Thai string in this product is a decision-log value. A snapshot would make an accidental copy change pass review as "just update the snapshot"; asserting the verbatim D-02/D-31/D-39 string makes the change visible in the diff.
  • No component tests. There are no components — the views are EJS rendered by the same process that owns the data, so tier 2 already renders them.

2.3 Environments

ADR §2.2 fixes two deployment environments — local and production — and refuses a staging environment on the grounds that a third environment is a third place for the webhook URL to be wrong. That stands. What QA adds is not an environment but two extra databases on the local machine and in CI, so a test run can truncate tables without destroying the demo a student is mid-way through rehearsing.

NameRuns whereDatabaseLINEDataReset by
local-devStudent laptop, npm run devjuristicline_test_mode = 1, webhook disabledseed.sqlnpm run db:reset, by hand
local-testnpm run test:intjuristic_testLINE_MOCK=1 — the fake client of spec §5.8.2schema.sql + per-test fixtures; the helper truncates between testsThe test helper, every run
local-e2enpm run test:e2ejuristic_e2eLINE_MOCK=1 + LINE_OUTBOX_FILEseed.sql once per run, then a per-scenario .sql fixturePlaywright globalSetup
ciGitHub Actionsjuristic_test in a mysql:8.4 service container, one per jobLINE_MOCK=1, never a real tokenRebuilt from empty in every jobThe job itself
productionRender + AivenjuristicReal channel, line_test_mode = 1 except during the rehearsed demoseed.sql, reseeded only from the Render shellNobody automatically — a manual, deliberate act
Three rules that are not negotiable
  • .env.test and .env.e2e point at juristic_test and juristic_e2e. The integration helper truncates every table it touches, so a DATABASE_URL pointing at juristic destroys a rehearsal. Both files are git-ignored and both are written fresh by CI.
  • No automated tier ever holds a real LINE_CHANNEL_ACCESS_TOKEN. CI sets LINE_MOCK=1 and a placeholder secret. The real token exists only in Render's environment and the team password note (D-43, R-12).
  • Nothing automated runs against production. The deployed instance is exercised by the §6.3.2 smoke test and by npm run rehearse pointed at its database, both by hand, both by a named person.

2.3.1 The deployed-instance smoke check

Two of this project's three third-party assets are fetched by the browser, not by the server — Chart.js from cdnjs and the Google Fonts stylesheet that carries Noto Sans Thai — and the engineering specification's helmet Content-Security-Policy directives are the only thing that lets them through. A CSP that is one origin too tight fails silently and only in the browser: the server returns 200, the HTML is perfect, the console carries a refusal nobody read, and the Dashboard shows four correct tiles above an empty rectangle where the chart should be. Nothing in tiers 1–3 can see that, so it is asserted by hand, on the deployed instance, as part of the smoke check — and it is the first thing to re-run after any change to server.js's middleware order or to a CDN URL (spec §6.3.2, 02 · Operations Runbook — deploy).

IDTitleTypePriPreconditionsStepsTest dataExpected resultTraces to
TC-AUTH-072 The deployed Dashboard loads Chart.js and the Google Fonts stylesheet with zero CSP violations UIP0 The deployed instance (or local-dev) is up and seeded; an admin session; a Chromium window with DevTools open, the Console filter set to All levels and both Console and Network cleared.
  1. Log in and open ภาพรวม at /.
  2. Read the Console tab top to bottom.
  3. Read the Network tab for the three external requests.
  4. Hard-reload with Disable cache ticked — a cold load is the demo-day case — and repeat steps 2 and 3.
  5. Repeat once at the 390 px mobile width of D-45.
 No fixture. The deployed origin and the seed's current period. Console: zero entries containing Content Security Policy, Refused to load, Refused to apply or Refused to connect — one is a red P0 even when the page looks right. Network: the cdnjs.cloudflare.com Chart.js bundle 200 and typeof window.Chart === 'function' in the console; the fonts.googleapis.com/css2 stylesheet 200; its fonts.gstatic.com font files 200. Rendered: the KPI tiles carry their figures, the chart canvas is drawn, not blank, and Thai text renders in Noto Sans Thai with no fallback boxes and no clipped upper vowels. Identical on the cold reload and at 390 px. FR-28, FR-47 · D-43, D-45, D-03

2.4 Test data and the demo seed fixture

db/seed.sql is the only test data this project has, and it is a deliverable rather than a convenience: D-44 makes it the thing that recreates the graded demo from an empty database in one command. Tests inherit two consequences. First, they may rely on the seed facts below and on nothing else. Second, every seed date is computed from CURDATE() — so a test that asserts 5 ก.ย. 2026 is a test that goes red on 6 September, and asserting the calendar is itself a defect.

Seed fact a test may rely onValueUsed bySource
Rooms80 across 5 floors, 101–116 … 501–516, every one 30.00 ตร.ม.E2E-01, E2E-06, FR-28D-44

Three figures no test may treat as expected values

D-44 is binding here and it is the easiest rule in the document to break by accident. PRD §16's 18,500 (water) and 62,500 (electricity) are illustrative and arithmetically impossible against the seed — 18,500 is not divisible by 18 and 62,500 is not divisible by 8 — and PRD §19's 35 ตร.ม. room row is illustrative in the same way. Only the room counts 80 / 67 / 13 and the common-area total 70,350.00 (67 × 30 × 35) are reproduced. A test asserting 18,500 is a defect in the test.

2.4.1 The demo fixture — test/fixtures/reset-205.sql

E2E-01 starts one step earlier than the seed does: the seed hands the demo a room 205 that is already read and already has a Draft bill (engineering specification §2.7 item 8), because the live demo only approves it. A test that must prove the reading step needs room 205 unread. This fixture re-arms it, in one file used by both tier 2 and tier 4, and it is the only fixture that touches the demo room.

This file has a demo-database twin, and they are not interchangeable

The same problem exists on a demo day: on a fresh npm run db:reset the first two actions of the demo script have nothing to do. The demo's answer is db/demo-reset.sql, run as npm run db:demo-arm (engineering specification §2.7, §6.2.4). It is this file's body minus the last statement — no UPDATE settings, so LINE test mode and line_test_user_id stay exactly as the operator left them.

That last statement is the whole reason there are two files. Whitelisting Utest0000000000000000000000000001 on a demo database would silently redirect every message the examiner is about to be shown to an id that does not exist. reset-205.sql is applied only to juristic_test and juristic_e2e; demo-reset.sql is applied only to a demo or production database. Neither is ever run against the other's database, and no automated tier ever runs db:demo-arm.

-- test/fixtures/reset-205.sql
-- Re-arms the PRD §28 demo on room 205: no reading, no bill, counter at 851.
-- Every date is derived from CURDATE(); nothing here hard-codes a calendar (D-44).
-- Applied ONLY to juristic_test / juristic_e2e. Never to a demo database.

SET @room   := (SELECT id FROM rooms WHERE room_code = '205');
SET @period := DATE_FORMAT(CURDATE(), '%Y-%m');
SET @prev   := DATE_FORMAT(CURDATE() - INTERVAL 1 MONTH, '%Y-%m');
SET @bill   := (SELECT id FROM bills WHERE room_id = @room AND period = @period);

-- Unwind the current period for this room, child rows first.
DELETE FROM notifications WHERE bill_id = @bill;
DELETE FROM receipts      WHERE payment_id IN (SELECT id FROM payments WHERE bill_id = @bill);
DELETE FROM payments      WHERE bill_id = @bill;
DELETE FROM bill_items    WHERE bill_id = @bill;
DELETE FROM audit_log     WHERE bill_id = @bill;
DELETE FROM bills         WHERE id      = @bill;
DELETE FROM meter_readings WHERE room_id = @room AND period = @period;

-- Pin last month's closing readings so the §28 arithmetic reproduces whatever
-- the seed's history generator produced. previous_reading is INT UNSIGNED, so the
-- subtraction is CAST to SIGNED and floored at 0 to satisfy CHECK (units >= 0).
UPDATE meter_readings
   SET current_reading = 1250,
       units = GREATEST(CAST(1250 AS SIGNED) - CAST(previous_reading AS SIGNED), 0)
 WHERE room_id = @room AND period = @prev AND utility_type = 'water';

UPDATE meter_readings
   SET current_reading = 5280,
       units = GREATEST(CAST(5280 AS SIGNED) - CAST(previous_reading AS SIGNED), 0)
 WHERE room_id = @room AND period = @prev AND utility_type = 'electric';

-- The counter is not gapless (D-26, D-27); it is reset so the next allocation is 852.
UPDATE counters SET seq = 851 WHERE name = 'receipt';

-- Whitelist a test recipient. seed.sql ships line_test_user_id NULL on purpose, which is
-- the SWALLOW branch of spec §5.6.5: with test mode ON and no whitelisted recipient the
-- sender makes NO API call, so the fake client is never invoked and the outbox stays
-- empty. Every outbox assertion in this plan would time out against that state (§2.5).
UPDATE settings SET line_test_user_id = 'Utest0000000000000000000000000001' WHERE id = 1;
Two deliberate properties of this fixture
  1. It mutates last month's closing reading and leaves last month's bill alone. That is correct, not sloppy: bill_items are snapshots taken at generation (D-09, D-17), so a Paid historical bill does not and must not recompute. The fixture is asserting the same invariant the product relies on.
  2. It resets the receipt counter to 851, not to 0. The 852 in REC-<YYYYMM>-000852 comes from the seed offset, and the sequence is unique and monotonic but explicitly not gapless (D-26, D-27). No test may assert gaplessness; the two assertions permitted are that no two receipts share a number and that a later payment never receives a lower one.

2.4.2 Fixtures the other scenarios need

FileWhat it sets upUsed by
reset-205.sqlRoom 205 unread, no bill, counter at 851, test recipient whitelistedE2E-01, E2E-07, the tier-2 generation test
reset-period.sqlUnwinds the whole current period: deletes its notifications, receipts, payments, bill_items, bills and period audit_log rows, then deletes the current-period meter_readings for rooms 108, 312 and 405 so the run has 64 read rooms and 3 unread. Dates from CURDATE(); idempotentTC-BILL-001 … 009, 031, 032, 049 · TC-AUTH-052 · TC-PAY-036
due-dates.sqlShifts due_date on six named rooms to CURDATE() + 2, − 1, − 8, − 15, − 3 and one Paid bill at − 1; voids one paymentE2E-02
next-period-bill.sqlAdds an approved bill for room 206 in the following period, so revert on the current one must be refusedE2E-03
unlink-206.sqlClears the three LINE columns on room 206's Active tenantE2E-05

Each is under twenty lines, each derives every date from CURDATE(), and each is idempotent — running it twice leaves the same state, so a rerun after a failed test needs no cleanup step. reset-period.sql is the one that is not merely additive: it takes the seed's shipped current period — 66 approved Unpaid bills plus room 205's Draft, all 67 rooms read — back to the state before a generation run, which is the state the generation, rate-recalculation and bulk-approval cases are written against. Without it those cases assert a period the seed never produces.

2.5 Mocking LINE

The Messaging API is mocked at exactly one seam, and the seam is the one the engineering spec already defines: the module-level client in services/notify.js with its setLineClient() setter, plus LINE_MOCK=1 which installs test/helpers/fakeLine.js at boot (spec §5.8.2). There is no HTTP mock server and no cassette library — that is an ADR-level choice, and this plan keeps it.

TierMechanismWhat is assertedWhy that surface
Unit setLineClient(fakeClient()) in the test body; a failing client is a two-line object whose pushMessage rejects with a given status. The outbox array: the rendered Thai text byte-for-byte against the D-31 worked example, and that a missing placeholder throws rather than emitting undefined. Template rendering is a pure function. Nothing else in the tier touches LINE.
API integration The real server booted with LINE_MOCK=1. The notifications rows, never the outbox: type, status, attempt_count, http_status, recipient_user_id, and that uq_notifications(dedupe_key) refuses a same-day repeat. The rows are what the Admin screens, the monthly quota counter and the reports read. Asserting them tests the thing that ships (spec §5.8.2).
Browser E2E LINE_MOCK=1 plus LINE_OUTBOX_FILE: the fake client appends one JSON line per outbound call to a file the Playwright process reads. Both halves — the text from the outbox file (the tenant's experience) and the row from MySQL (the office's experience), asserted together. Playwright drives the browser, not the server process, so it cannot reach the in-process outbox array. A file is the smallest bridge across that boundary.
Inbound webhook A signed request posted by test/helpers/lineWebhook.js, signing the exact bytes it sends. 200 on a valid signature and 401 on a tampered one; the line_followers upsert; the binding, the expired code and the TENANT_LINE_LINK audit row. The LINE console holds exactly one webhook URL and it belongs to the deployment (D-43), so inbound events can only be exercised by signed fixtures.

2.5.1 The four-line change to test/helpers/fakeLine.js

// test/helpers/fakeLine.js — spec §5.8.2, plus the outbox file the browser tier reads.
import { appendFileSync } from 'node:fs';

export const fakeClient = () => {
  const outbox = [];
  const record = (e) => {
    outbox.push(e);
    if (process.env.LINE_OUTBOX_FILE) appendFileSync(process.env.LINE_OUTBOX_FILE, JSON.stringify(e) + '\n');
    return { status: 200 };
  };
  return {
    outbox,
    pushMessage:  async (to, msg, key) => record({ api: 'push',      to, msg, key }),
    multicast:    async (to, msg, key) => record({ api: 'multicast', to, msg, key }),
    replyMessage: async (token, msg)  => record({ api: 'reply',      token, msg }),
  };
};

A JSONL file rather than an HTTP mock server, for three reasons: it costs four lines instead of a second process and a port; it cannot drift from the client the integration tier already uses, because it is that client; and it never changes what the server does, so a passing E2E is evidence about the shipped code path and not about a test double. The rejected alternative — a fake LINE server on 127.0.0.1 with LINE_API_BASE pointed at it — would additionally exercise the SDK's own HTTP layer, which is not code this team wrote and not code that can break the demo.

Every automated tier must whitelist a test recipient first — the seed's NULL is the no-API swallow path

db/seed.sql ships settings.line_test_user_id = NULL deliberately (engineering specification §2.7), and §5.6.5 makes that value the swallow branch: with line_test_mode = 1 and no whitelisted recipient the sender makes no API call at all and writes the row status='sent', attempt_count=1, http_status NULL, last_error='test mode, no whitelisted recipient', charging nothing to the monthly 500. The fake client of §2.5.1 is therefore never invoked, LINE_OUTBOX_FILE stays empty, and every outbox.waitForText(…) in this plan throws after 15 seconds — including the one in E2E-01, the scenario that may never go red.

So every automated tier sets a whitelisted test recipient before it asserts an outbox. test/fixtures/reset-205.sql carries the statement, test/e2e/global-setup.ts runs it after db:reset, and the tier-2 helper test/helpers/db.js runs it in its own setup:

UPDATE settings SET line_test_user_id = 'Utest0000000000000000000000000001' WHERE id = 1;

The swallow branch itself is not thereby untested — TC-AUTH-071 asserts it against the untouched seed, which is the state a fresh clone and the §5.8.1 / §6.5.1 rehearsals actually run in.

Test mode changes who receives, not what is recorded

With a recipient whitelisted, line_test_mode (ON in every environment, D-34) redirects every outbound message to that one userId while still recording the intended recipient on the row. So in every automated tier the outbox entry's to is the test account and the notifications.recipient_user_id is the tenant. A test that asserts the recipient must read the database row; a test that asserts the message text may read the outbox. Getting this backwards produces a test that passes with test mode off and fails with it on — the exact opposite of what is wanted.

2.5.2 Signed webhook events — test/helpers/lineWebhook.js

// test/helpers/lineWebhook.js
// The signature is base64(HMAC-SHA256(rawBody, channelSecret)) over the EXACT bytes sent.
// Reformatting the JSON changes the signature, so the body is stringified once and reused.
import { createHmac } from 'node:crypto';

export const sign = (rawBody, secret = process.env.LINE_CHANNEL_SECRET) =>
  createHmac('sha256', secret).update(rawBody).digest('base64');

const DESTINATION = 'Uoa' + '0'.repeat(30);

export const followEvent = (userId) => ({
  type: 'follow', mode: 'active', timestamp: Date.now(),
  source: { type: 'user', userId },
  webhookEventId: '01JTESTFOLLOW', deliveryContext: { isRedelivery: false },
  replyToken: '0'.repeat(32),
});

export const messageEvent = (userId, text) => ({
  type: 'message', mode: 'active', timestamp: Date.now(),
  source: { type: 'user', userId },
  webhookEventId: '01JTESTMSG', deliveryContext: { isRedelivery: false },
  replyToken: '1'.repeat(32),
  message: { id: '1', type: 'text', quoteToken: 'q1', text },
});

/** POSTs one signed event. Pass tamper: true to assert the 401 path. */
export async function postEvent(baseUrl, event, { tamper = false } = {}) {
  const body = JSON.stringify({ destination: DESTINATION, events: [event] });
  return fetch(baseUrl + '/line/webhook', {
    method: 'POST',
    headers: {
      'Content-Type': 'application/json',
      'X-Line-Signature': tamper ? 'bm90LWEtc2lnbmF0dXJl' : sign(body),
    },
    body,
  });
}

The shell equivalent is spec §5.8.3 and stays in 02 · Operations Runbook — the webhook URL for hand-testing against the deployed instance. Two properties matter and are asserted: the endpoint answers 200 before doing any work (D-29, D-43 — LINE disables an endpoint that repeatedly fails to answer, and a disabled webhook silently kills the only route by which a userId ever reaches the system), and a tampered signature is 401 with nothing written. A fabricated replyToken is harmless under LINE_MOCK=1; against the real dev channel it would fail with 400 while the binding — the part under test — still happens.

2.6 Clock control for due and overdue tests

The clock cannot be frozen, and trying is the wrong answer

Overdue is produced by CASE WHEN b.status = 'Unpaid' AND b.due_date < CURDATE() THEN 'Overdue' ELSE b.status END — evaluated by MySQL, on the database server, at query time (D-21). A JavaScript fake timer cannot move CURDATE(). Worse, it would desynchronise todayBangkok() from CURDATE() and put the system into a state it can never actually reach, so the test would be asserting fiction.

The technique is to move the data, not the clock. One UPDATE per bill, expressed relative to CURDATE(), and both the SQL expression and the JS helper agree because both still read a real, consistent today.

-- Any bill can be aged or brought forward with one statement.
UPDATE bills SET due_date = CURDATE() + INTERVAL 2 DAY WHERE id = ?;  -- PRE_DUE fires today
UPDATE bills SET due_date = CURDATE() - INTERVAL 1 DAY WHERE id = ?;  -- displays Overdue; days_overdue = 1
UPDATE bills SET due_date = CURDATE() - INTERVAL 8 DAY WHERE id = ?;  -- days_overdue = 8
ApproachVerdictReason
Shift due_date relative to CURDATE()chosenOne statement, no dependency, moves the only variable that actually decides the outcome, and leaves JS and SQL agreeing on what today is.
@sinonjs/fake-timers or an injected clockrejectedA dev dependency that cannot reach CURDATE(). It would make billStatus() disagree with the SQL expression — the precise defect D-21's single-helper rule exists to prevent.
Playwright's page.clockrejectedMoves the browser's clock. Every status, due date and amount in this system is computed server-side and no date arithmetic happens in the browser at all (ADR §6.5).
libfaketime or a time-travelling containerrejectedDays of setup, a class of flake all of its own, and it would move the receipt month too — silently invalidating REC-<YYYYMM>-000852.
A TEST_TODAY environment override read by the apprejectedProduction code branching on a test variable is a defect waiting for the one deploy where the variable is set. If it existed, its own failure mode would need a test.

2.6.1 Two consequences that catch people out

  1. The reminder ladder needs six bills, not six clock jumps. notifications.dedupe_key includes send_date, and send_date is today (D-30, ENG-3). Aging one bill to −1, running the job, then aging the same bill to −8 and running again produces the same dedupe key and UPSERTs the same row — no second message, correctly. So D-33's ladder is proved with one job run against six differently-aged bills, which is also how the production system actually behaves on any given morning.
  2. The daily job will not run twice in one day, by design. uq_job_runs(job_name, run_date) makes a second daily call a no-op returning already_ran: true (D-32). A test that needs a second run either deletes the row — DELETE FROM job_runs WHERE job_name = 'daily' AND run_date = CURDATE(); — or uses the Settings path, which runs the identical code as daily_manual and deliberately skips the guard. Both are asserted: the no-op is the requirement (FR-37).
Defect candidate raised by writing the due-date oracle · S3 · documentation

D-20 and ADR §4 both state due_date = MAX(day-of-month of the period month, approval_date + 3 days), "clamped to the month's last day". Read literally, an approval on 30 September for period 2026-09 gives MAX(5 Sep, 3 Oct) = 3 Oct, which the clamp would then pull back to 30 September — a due date before the three-day floor D-20 exists to guarantee, and one day after approval. The reading that preserves the intent is that the clamp applies to the day-of-month term only (it exists for a due_day that a short month lacks, which the 1–28 range already prevents), and never to the result of the MAX. QA implements the oracle that way, asserts it in test/unit/dueDate.test.js, and asks for one clarifying clause in D-20 and ADR §4. Until that clause exists, a late approval near month end is the one place the product and this test plan could legitimately disagree.

2.7 Entry and exit criteria

Entry criteria — a build QA will accept for testing

  • npm run lint is green and npm ci installs from the committed lock file.
  • npm run db:reset completes and prints 80 rooms · 67 tenants · 54 LINE-linked · rates 18.00/8.00/35.00 · due day 5. A seed that prints anything else is rejected before a single test is run.
  • npm run test:unit is green — a red unit tier means the arithmetic is wrong and no higher tier's result is meaningful.
  • GET /healthz returns {"data":{"ok":true,"db":"up"}} on the target instance.
  • settings.line_test_mode = 1, verified by reading the Settings page, not by trusting the default.
  • The pull request names the decision ids it implements and says "requires db:reset" on the first line when the schema or seed changed.

Exit criteria — per milestone

  • Every FR in the milestone's scope has at least one test that names its id, and the traceability grep of §2.9 reports zero uncovered FRs for that milestone.
  • Zero open S1. Zero open S2 on the demo path (Login → จดมิเตอร์ → generate → อนุมัติและส่งบิล → LINE BILL → ยืนยันรับเงินสด → receipt → LINE PAID → Dashboard).
  • All four tiers green in CI on the merge commit, not only on a developer's laptop.
  • npm run rehearse green against the milestone's own database, with its six §28 numbers pasted into the milestone note.
  • The five D-45 numbers measured with a stopwatch against the full seed and recorded, even when one of them misses: an unmet target is a known figure, not a silent one.
  • Every new Thai string checked verbatim against its decision-log entry. A reworded confirmation dialog is a D-39 defect, not a copy edit.

Exit criteria — the defence build, additionally

  • The A5 ใบเสร็จรับเงิน printed on the office printer within the last seven days, one page, Thai unbroken, margins intact (R-06).
  • The webhook re-verified green in the LINE console after the final deploy (D-43 — the one runbook step that has silently killed the linking flow).
  • The local fallback rehearsed for real: local app, local MySQL restored from the newest dump, webhook disabled, test mode ON (R-10).
  • A mysqldump committed the day before, and the demo account's password known to two people.

2.8 Defect severity

SeverityDefinitionExamples from this systemResponse
S1 · Demo-stopping A wrong number reaches a human, money or state can be lost or double-taken, a role boundary leaks, or the demo path cannot be completed at all. A total that is not the sum of its printed lines · a second ยืนยันรับเงินสด creating a second payment or receipt · an amount in a Staff response body · a bill approved with fewer than three bill_items · a money change committed with no audit_log row · a date rendered in Buddhist Era · the webhook answering anything but 200 · Overdue written into bills.status. Stop other work. Fixed before the next merge. A regression test is part of the fix, not a follow-up.
S2 · Major A specified flow fails or is blocked, but a documented workaround exists and no number is wrong. ตีกลับเป็นฉบับร่าง refused when it should be allowed · a CSV without its UTF-8 BOM so Excel mangles Thai · the drain loop stopping with remaining > 0 · a report's bucket counts not summing to its row count · a confirmation dialog missing the count or the irreversibility sentence. Fixed inside the same milestone. Blocks the milestone sign-off if it sits on the demo path.
S3 · Minor Wrong or missing Thai copy, a missing empty state, an unhelpful error, an ambiguity in a binding document. A list without its D-40 empty state · ตกลง on a primary button where D-39 requires the verb · a 422 whose Thai message differs from the schema's · the D-20 clamp ambiguity raised in §2.6. Batched and fixed within the milestone. Copy defects are fixed by quoting the decision-log entry in the pull request.
S4 · Cosmetic Visual polish with no functional or comprehension impact. Spacing off the 4px scale · a badge one shade wrong · a column narrower than it should be at 1280 px. Fixed if a milestone has slack. Never blocks anything.
Four escalation rules that override the table
  1. Any defect that changes a number a tenant sees is S1, however small the delta and however unlikely the path. 1,790.00 versus 1,789.99 is not a rounding preference.
  2. A missing or wrong audit_log row on a money or bill-state change is S1 even when the amount is correct. D-38 makes the trail part of the transaction; an untraceable correct number is an untestable system.
  3. Any role leak is S1. A Staff response containing a rate, an amount or a total is S1 whether or not a template hides it, because D-36 is about the response body.
  4. Anything that could spend the LINE allowance is at least S2 — a send inside a transaction, a broadcast, a per-tenant push for an announcement, or test mode defaulting off (D-34, R-02). There is no second pool of 500 messages.

2.9 Traceability

Traceability is maintained by naming, not by a spreadsheet that rots. Every test title begins with the requirement ids it covers, so the mapping lives in the test file and can never drift from the test that implements it.

// Tier 1 and 2 — node:test
test('FR-17 · D-18 · a second generation run creates nothing and recalculates Drafts', …);

// Tier 4 — Playwright
test('E2E-01 · FR-10 FR-11 FR-12 FR-13 FR-14 FR-15 FR-16 FR-17 FR-18 FR-19 FR-22 FR-23 FR-24 FR-28 · …', …);

Coverage is then a grep, run as npm run trace, and it needs no library:

# package.json → "trace": "node --test --test-reporter=tap 2>/dev/null | grep -oE 'FR-[0-9]{2}' | sort -u > .tmp/covered.txt
#                 && seq -f 'FR-%02g' 1 48 | grep -vxFf .tmp/covered.txt || echo 'all 48 FRs covered'"

The finished matrix this rule produces — every FR and every decision against the cases that discharge it — is §8. npm run trace prints the FR ids that no test title mentions. That is the whole tool: it cannot tell a good test from a bad one, but it reliably catches the requirement nobody wrote a test for, which is the failure mode a coverage percentage hides rather than reveals.

E2E idFunctional requirements coveredDecisions exercisedTier-2 partner test
E2E-01FR-01, FR-10 … FR-19, FR-22, FR-23, FR-24, FR-28, FR-32, FR-40D-12, D-13, D-15, D-16, D-17, D-18, D-19, D-20, D-24, D-26, D-27, D-30, D-31, D-34, D-39, D-41, D-44bills.generate.test.js, payments.confirm.test.js
E2E-02FR-20, FR-21, FR-23, FR-32, FR-35, FR-37D-21, D-25, D-30, D-32, D-33jobs.daily.test.js

E2E-06FR-27, FR-32, FR-46D-30, D-34, D-35, D-39announcements.send.test.js
E2E-07FR-17, FR-18, FR-22, FR-42D-18, D-23, D-26bills.generate.test.js, payments.double.test.js

FRs no E2E scenario covers — FR-03 … FR-09, FR-25, FR-26, FR-29, FR-30, FR-31, FR-33, FR-36, FR-41, FR-43, FR-44, FR-45, FR-47 — are covered at tiers 1 and 2, where they belong. A CRUD form and a CSV BOM do not need a browser.

2.10 Test hooks — five attributes, and no more

The rule, and why it is four rather than forty

D-02 already establishes the principle: every element rendering a status carries data-status="Draft|Unpaid|Paid|Overdue" so tests assert on the English identifier while the human reads Thai. Four attributes extend the same principle to the four other things a test must identify without reading Thai copy — or a number format — the office is free to reword. Every one is rendered by a partial that ADR §5.2 already lists, so the cost is three lines across two files.

AttributeOnRendered byStatus
data-status="Draft|Unpaid|Paid|Overdue"Every element showing a bill statusviews/partials/status-badge.ejsalready binding (D-02)
data-room="205"Any row or card representing one roomList and card partialsrequested here
data-bill="1842"Any row or element representing one billList and detail viewsrequested here
data-amount="water|electric|common|total"Every rendered money valueviews/partials/money.ejsrequested here
data-previous="1250"The read-only ครั้งก่อน value on a reading card — the unformatted integerThe reading-card partialrequested here

Everything else is located the way a user locates it — by accessible role and name, using the Thai labels D-02, D-39 and the design document fix verbatim (บันทึก, อนุมัติและส่งบิล, ยืนยันรับเงินสด, พิมพ์ใบเสร็จ). Those strings are decision-log values: if one changes, a red test is the correct outcome, because the change needs a decision-log amendment. No data-testid is introduced anywhere; a page needing one is a page whose semantics are wrong, and fixing the semantics also fixes the screen reader (design §9).

The fifth exists because D-06 makes ครั้งก่อน read-only text, never an input, and TC-ROOM-042 requires it rendered with thousands separators. A test therefore cannot reach it with toHaveValue — that applies only to form controls — and asserting the rendered 1,250 couples the scenario to a number format. data-previous carries the raw 1250 alongside it, so both halves are assertable and only the one the case cares about breaks.

One further requirement falls out of the reading round: each utility block on a reading card is a <fieldset> with a <legend> of น้ำ or ไฟ, so that the two ครั้งนี้ inputs on one card are distinguishable by accessible name. That is an accessibility fix the design document's §9 wants anyway; it is recorded here because a test depends on it.

2.11 Continuous integration

This is the only .github/workflows/ci.yml

The engineering specification's §6.5.3 no longer carries a workflow file. It carries the seven gates the file must satisfy and the two ordering rules below, and points here; the YAML lives in one place, and that place is this section. To change CI, change this file. M0's exit condition — ".github/workflows/ci.yml green in week 2" — means the verify and api jobs below; the e2e job joins them in M8 when tier 4 is written, and until then it is present and skipped by an empty test/e2e/.

Three jobs, in the same spirit as the single job the specification used to describe: gates, not decoration. verify is fast and needs no database, so a lint error or a broken formula fails in under a minute; api and e2e each get their own MySQL 8.4 service container, so neither can leave state for the other.

# .github/workflows/ci.yml
name: ci
on: [push, pull_request]

jobs:
  # Gate 1 — lint and the pure tier. No database, under a minute.
  verify:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with: { node-version: '24', cache: 'npm' }
      - run: npm ci
      - run: npm run lint
      - run: npm run test:unit

  # Gate 2 — the schema applies to an empty database, and the races that lose money.
  api:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    needs: verify
    services:
      mysql:
        image: mysql:8.4
        env:
          MYSQL_ROOT_PASSWORD: root
          MYSQL_DATABASE: juristic_test
        ports: ['3306:3306']
        options: >-
          --health-cmd="mysqladmin ping -proot"
          --health-interval=5s --health-timeout=5s --health-retries=20
    env:
      DATABASE_URL: mysql://root:root@127.0.0.1:3306/juristic_test
      SESSION_SECRET: ci-only-not-a-secret
      SEED_ADMIN_PASSWORD: ci-only-not-a-secret
      LINE_CHANNEL_SECRET: ci-only-not-a-secret
      LINE_CHANNEL_ACCESS_TOKEN: ci-only-not-a-secret
      JOB_TOKEN: ci-only-not-a-secret
      LINE_MOCK: '1'
      NODE_ENV: test
      TZ: Asia/Bangkok
      PORT: '3000'
      TEST_BASE_URL: http://127.0.0.1:3000
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with: { node-version: '24', cache: 'npm' }
      - run: npm ci
      # db:migrate and db:seed read --env-file=.env, and test:int reads --env-file=.env.test,
      # so BOTH files are written before either script runs. Writing them afterwards is the
      # ENOENT that spec §6.5.3 half-fixes (it writes .env.test but not .env).
      - name: Write env files
        run: |
          env | grep -E '^(DATABASE_URL|SESSION_SECRET|SEED_ADMIN_PASSWORD|LINE_CHANNEL_SECRET|LINE_CHANNEL_ACCESS_TOKEN|JOB_TOKEN|LINE_MOCK|NODE_ENV|TZ|PORT|TEST_BASE_URL)=' > .env
          cp .env .env.test
      - run: npm run db:migrate      # gate: schema.sql applies to an empty database
      - run: npm run db:seed         # gate: seed.sql rebuilds the graded demo
      # Tier 2 drives the app over HTTP with fetch (§7.9), so unlike the e2e job — whose
      # playwright.config.ts declares a webServer — this job has to start the server itself.
      # Without these two lines every tier-2 case fails ECONNREFUSED and the gate the plan
      # calls "the races that lose money" never actually runs.
      - name: Start the app for the integration tier
        run: |
          node --env-file=.env.test server.js &
          npx --yes wait-port 127.0.0.1:3000
      - run: npm run test:int

  # Gate 3 — the seven browser scenarios. Chromium only, one worker, no retries.
  e2e:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    needs: api
    services:
      mysql:
        image: mysql:8.4
        env:
          MYSQL_ROOT_PASSWORD: root
          MYSQL_DATABASE: juristic_e2e
        ports: ['3306:3306']
        options: >-
          --health-cmd="mysqladmin ping -proot"
          --health-interval=5s --health-timeout=5s --health-retries=20
    env:
      DATABASE_URL: mysql://root:root@127.0.0.1:3306/juristic_e2e
      SESSION_SECRET: ci-only-not-a-secret
      SEED_ADMIN_PASSWORD: ci-only-not-a-secret
      LINE_CHANNEL_SECRET: ci-only-not-a-secret
      LINE_CHANNEL_ACCESS_TOKEN: ci-only-not-a-secret
      JOB_TOKEN: ci-only-not-a-secret
      LINE_MOCK: '1'
      LINE_OUTBOX_FILE: .tmp/line-outbox.jsonl
      E2E_BASE_URL: http://127.0.0.1:3100
      NODE_ENV: test
      TZ: Asia/Bangkok
      PORT: '3100'
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with: { node-version: '24', cache: 'npm' }
      - run: npm ci
      - run: npx playwright install --with-deps chromium
      - name: Write env files
        run: |
          mkdir -p .tmp
          env | grep -E '^(DATABASE_URL|SESSION_SECRET|SEED_ADMIN_PASSWORD|LINE_CHANNEL_SECRET|LINE_CHANNEL_ACCESS_TOKEN|JOB_TOKEN|LINE_MOCK|LINE_OUTBOX_FILE|NODE_ENV|TZ|PORT)=' > .env
          cp .env .env.e2e
      - run: npm run db:reset
      - run: npx playwright test
      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        if: failure()
        with:
          name: playwright-report
          path: .tmp/playwright-report
          retention-days: 7
Defect found while writing this pipeline · S2 · CI · closed

The specification's earlier workflow ran npm run db:migrate before the printf that created .env.test, and explained the printf as a fix for npm test's missing env file. But db:migrate is node --env-file=.env db/reset.js --schema-only — it reads .env, not .env.test, and that job never wrote .env at all, so Node exited ENOENT on the schema step and the gate never ran. The same job also never started the server, so the integration tier it claimed to gate would have failed ECONNREFUSED. Both are fixed above and the duplicate file is deleted: this workflow writes both env files as the first step after npm ci and starts the app before npm run test:int. Recorded as §9.3 #8, now closed.

Four things this pipeline deliberately does not have: a coverage gate (ADR §6.9), a matrix over Node versions (the project pins one), a deploy step (Render auto-deploys on green main), and any secret. Every value above is the literal string ci-only-not-a-secret, which is safe precisely because LINE_MOCK=1 means no credential is ever used against a real endpoint.

2.12 Assumptions recorded by this section

  1. Playwright is in devDependencies under the A-9 amendment, granted as set out in §2.2 and written into engineering specification §6.5.1 at a cost of 2 person-days in M8. No longer an assumption; recorded here because the identifiers it introduces are listed in item 5.
  2. Four test hooks — data-room, data-bill, data-amount and data-previous — are added alongside D-02's existing data-status, rendered by partials ADR §5.2 already lists. This asks for one row in the UI document's §8.4 table.
  3. Each utility block on a reading card is a <fieldset> with a <legend> (น้ำ / ไฟ), so the two ครั้งนี้ inputs on one card have distinct accessible names.
  4. Three pure helpers are exported from services/billing.js — resolvePreviousReading(rows, opts), computeUnits(input) and resolveRate(rateRows, period) — each taking rows as arguments and touching no database, which is what makes ADR §6.9's unit list literally implementable. Their SQL callers are unchanged and stay in the service.
  5. New identifiers introduced here: the database juristic_e2e; the files .env.e2e, playwright.config.ts, test/e2e/**, test/fixtures/*.sql, test/helpers/lineWebhook.js; the npm scripts test:e2e and trace; the environment variables LINE_OUTBOX_FILE and TEST_BASE_URL (default http://127.0.0.1:3000, read by test/helpers/client.js and set by the api CI job); the severity ids S1–S4; the scenario ids E2E-01 … E2E-07.
  6. The staff account's password. D-44 names SEED_ADMIN_PASSWORD for the admin and is silent on staff. The E2E helper reads E2E_STAFF_PASSWORD and falls back to SEED_ADMIN_PASSWORD, so it works whether seed.sql hashes one value for both users or two.
  7. The D-20 clamp is read as applying to the day-of-month term only (§2.6). Raised as an S3 documentation defect against D-20 and ADR §4.
  8. Amounts are asserted against the database or a rendered page, never recomputed in JavaScript by production code. Test code may compute an expected value — that is what an oracle is — but a unit test cannot verify ROUND(quantity × unit_price, 2) because the product performs it in SQL (A-4). The three money formulas are therefore proved in the DB-backed tier, and the pure tier proves the quantities that feed them.
  9. The Playwright tier runs single-worker and non-parallel against one MySQL instance. Parallelising would need one database per worker, which buys nothing at seven scenarios.
  10. Fictional data only. Every name, phone number and LINE id in every fixture is fictional and seeded, per D-44. No real tenant data exists anywhere in this project.
  11. Every automated tier whitelists line_test_user_id = 'Utest0000000000000000000000000001' before asserting an outbox, because the seed's NULL is the no-API swallow branch of specification §5.6.5 (§2.5). The id is fictional and never a valid push target; under LINE_MOCK=1 nothing leaves the process. The swallow branch itself is asserted against the untouched seed by TC-AUTH-071.

3. Authentication, Users, Roles & Settings

Seventy-one executable test cases covering the gate and the control room of Juristic Platform: sign-in and session lifetime, the two-role permission matrix on every page and endpoint group, the Users page, and the four Settings forms whose values decide what a future bill costs and what a receipt prints. Cases are grouped by feature; ids run TC-AUTH-001 … TC-AUTH-071 in one continuous sequence, so an id never moves when a group grows.

The nine columns, the four case types and the three priorities are defined once in §1.2, and the seed the cases run against in §1.3. Only what is specific to this section is repeated below.

3.1 Fixture and preconditions specific to this section

The standing fixture of §1.3 applies unchanged — rebuilt database, TZ=Asia/Bangkok, settings.line_test_mode = 1. What this section adds is the Settings seed the rate, due-date and apartment cases read, including one deliberately future-dated electricity rate.

Settings seed

Rates
น้ำ 18.00 · ไฟ 8.00 · ส่วนกลาง 35.00, all effective_from 2025-10-01
Future rate
ไฟ 8.50 effective_from 2026-10-01
วันครบกำหนด
due_day = 5
Apartment
ศุภาลัย เรสซิเดนซ์ · 99/1 ถนนพหลโยธิน แขวงจตุจักร เขตจตุจักร กรุงเทพมหานคร 10900 · โทร 021234567 · เลขประจำตัวผู้เสียภาษี — ไม่ได้ตั้งค่า · เวลาทำการ จันทร์-ศุกร์ 09:00-17:00 น.
LINE
line_test_mode = 1, line_test_user_id NULL as seeded; the cases below that assert an outbox whitelist a recipient first (§2.5), and TC-AUTH-071 asserts the untouched NULL state.

These are the seed's settings row verbatim (engineering specification §2.7). tax_id is NULL as shipped, so no เลขประจำตัวผู้เสียภาษี line is the default letterhead and a printed one is the variant.

Four contradictions between the source documents, and how these tests resolve them

Each resolution is a QA decision, not an invention; the losing variant is recorded here so the owning author can correct their document.

  1. Inactive account message. Engineering spec §3.3.1 and the error catalogue make wrong password, unknown username and Inactive user indistinguishable (LOGIN_INVALID · ชื่อผู้ใช้หรือรหัสผ่านไม่ถูกต้อง); UI design §1 gives the Inactive case its own banner บัญชีนี้ถูกปิดการใช้งาน. Tests follow the spec — it is the binding rules document and the security-conservative option (no account enumeration). TC-AUTH-006 asserts the shared message and flags the design's banner as a documentation defect to reconcile.
  2. Forced first-password change. D-37 states that "first login forces a change", which reads as a gate; the ADR's users table carries no must_change_password column to drive one, and engineering spec §3.7(8) resolves the wording explicitly — the rule is realised as GET/POST /password plus a runbook deploy step and a Dashboard banner, not as a forced redirect. The UI design has no /password/change screen and no forced-change state, so it agrees with the spec. Tests follow the ADR and spec: TC-AUTH-019…021 exercise /password with current_password. No test asserts a forced-redirect flow, and none may be written unless a column is added to the schema first.
  3. Duplicate-username message. Spec catalogue: ชื่อผู้ใช้นี้ถูกใช้แล้ว. UI design: ชื่อผู้ใช้นี้มีอยู่แล้ว. Tests assert the spec string (§3.1.6 declares itself exhaustive); TC-AUTH-037 records the design variant.
  4. Failed-login HTTP status. Spec §3.3.1 says the page surface re-renders with 200; the error catalogue maps LOGIN_INVALID to 401. Tests assert the page contract — re-render with the Thai banner, no Set-Cookie session, values preserved — and treat 200 (page) / 401 (JSON) as the two surfaces of one rule.
Assumptions recorded by this section
  1. Session assertions inspect the sessions table directly, because express-mysql-session is the only durable evidence that a session was created or destroyed; there is no GET /api/me to ask (spec §3.1.3).
  2. "No amount appears in a Staff response body" is asserted mechanically: the response text is searched for /\d{1,3}(,\d{3})*\.\d{2}/ and for the strings บาท, unit_price, total, amount. A hit is a failure even when the number is unrelated (ADR §6.9, D-36).
  3. Rate-resolution and due-date cases are written as unit tests over fixtures because both are pure functions of (period, rows) and (due_day, approval_date); the e2e cases that follow each one prove the same rule through HTTP.
  4. The demo calendar (5 ก.ย. 2026, 3 ก.ย. 2026, REC-202609-000852) is used as documentation values only. No clock is ever pinned — §2.6 rejects every mechanism that could pin one, and the technique is to move the data, not the clock. Where a case depends on "today" it either shifts a date relative to CURDATE() or computes the expectation from the expectedDueDate() / todayBangkok() oracles of §7.8, per D-44: reproduce the arithmetic, never the calendar.
  5. The login rate limiter is exercised with the limiter store reset between cases; otherwise ten earlier failures in one file would poison the next case.

TC-AUTH-008 Eleventh failed attempt from one IP inside 15 minutes is rate-limited APIP1 Limiter store empty; app.set('trust proxy', 1) configured
  1. Send 10 failed POST /login from IP 203.0.113.9
  2. Send an 11th, still wrong
  3. Send a 12th with the correct admin password from the same IP
  4. Send one correct login from IP 203.0.113.10
 X-Forwarded-For: 203.0.113.9 ×11 · admin/wrong-pass, then admin/Passw0rd!2026 Attempts 1–10: LOGIN_INVALID. Attempt 11: 429 LOGIN_RATE_LIMITED whose envelope message is the specification catalogue's เข้าสู่ระบบผิดพลาดหลายครั้ง กรุณารออีก 15 นาที — the catalogue declares itself exhaustive and is the tie-breaker this section adopts in contradictions 1, 3 and 5, so the design's page banner พยายามเข้าสู่ระบบไม่สำเร็จหลายครั้งเกินไป / กรุณารอ 15 นาที แล้วลองใหม่อีกครั้ง is not asserted and is recorded as contradiction 12 in §9.3. Primary button .btn.is-disabled. Attempt 12 is also 429 and no bcrypt comparison runs — the hash is never touched. The other IP signs in normally (303 → /dashboard), proving the limiter keys on the real client IP and not on the proxy. FR-01 · D-37
TC-AUTH-009 Login POST without a valid CSRF token is refused APIP1 A CSRF token was issued on GET /login
  1. POST /login omitting _csrf
  2. POST /login with _csrf from a different session
  3. Read sessions
 Valid credentials · _csrf absent, then _csrf=deadbeef… Both attempts 403 CSRF_INVALID — แบบฟอร์มหมดอายุ กรุณาโหลดหน้าใหม่แล้วลองอีกครั้ง. No session is created even though the credentials are correct. Comparison uses crypto.timingSafeEqual. FR-01 · D-37 · ADR A-8
TC-AUTH-010 An already-signed-in user opening /login is sent to their own landing page UIP2 Two browsers: one signed in as admin, one as staff
  1. As admin, GET /login
  2. As staff, GET /login
  3. As staff, GET /
 — Admin: 303 → /dashboard. Staff: 303 → /readings in both steps. No banner, no error page — Login has no 403 state because it is the only route needing no session. FR-01, FR-02 · D-36
TC-AUTH-011 Session cookie carries the decided flags APIP1 App started once with NODE_ENV=production behind TLS, once with NODE_ENV=development
  1. POST /login as admin in each environment
  2. Parse the Set-Cookie header
 Valid admin credentials Cookie name connect.sid (library default, not renamed); attributes HttpOnly and SameSite=Lax in both environments; Secure present in production and absent in development. No JWT, no bearer token, no Authorization header and nothing written to localStorage. FR-01 · D-37
TC-AUTH-012 Passwords are bcrypt-hashed at cost 10 and never leave the server unitP1 User created through POST /users
  1. Read users.password_hash for the new user
  2. Assert the bcrypt prefix and cost
  3. Grep every login and users response body and the rendered HTML for the plaintext
 password=Passw0rd!2026 password_hash is 60 characters and starts $2a$10$ or $2b$10$; it is not the plaintext and not a hex digest. The plaintext appears in no response body, no HTML value= attribute and no log line; the password input is never re-populated after any failure. FR-01, FR-03 · D-37

3.4 Changing your own password (GET/POST /password)

These three cases discharge FR-49, which the engineering specification added after this section was written: the screen was specified in §3.2.1 and §3.3.1, tested here, and named by no requirement, appeared in no milestone and had no mockup. It now has all four — specification §1.5 FR-49 and M0, UI design's เปลี่ยนรหัสผ่าน screen and its account-block link in both navigations. TC-AUTH-027's href set already included /password and is unchanged.

TC-AUTH-021 New password shorter than 8 characters, or a mismatched confirmation, is rejected UIP2 Signed in as admin
  1. Submit a 7-character new password
  2. Submit an 8-character new password with a different confirmation
 new=Short1! (7) — then new=Chang3d!2026, confirm=Chang3d!2025 Both 422, no hash change. Messages, verbatim: รหัสผ่านต้องมีอย่างน้อย 8 ตัวอักษร and รหัสผ่านไม่ตรงกัน. On the mismatch only the confirmation field carries .field.invalid; both password values are cleared and focus returns to the confirmation. FR-49, FR-01, FR-48 · D-37, D-40

3.8 Settings — rates, effective-from dates and history

The rate form is the only control in the product that changes what a future bill costs. Every case here either proves that history is preserved or that an approved bill is immune.

TC-AUTH-048 Rate resolution is keyed to the first day of the period month, not to the click unitP0 Fixture rows only; no HTTP
  1. Resolve each utility for 2026-09

Arithmetic that follows: 10 × 18.00 = 180.00, 70 × 8.00 = 560.00, 30 × 35.00 = 1,050.00, total 1,790.00. FR-07, FR-08, FR-09, FR-14, FR-15, FR-16 · D-17, D-16

TC-AUTH-053 Invalid rate values are rejected before they can reach a bill UIP2 Signed in as admin
  1. Submit each invalid value in turn
  2. Assert the message and the row count
 0 · -5.00 · 18.005 · abc · empty · effective_from= empty All 422 VALIDATION_FAILED, no row inserted, values preserved. Messages: อัตราต้องมากกว่า 0 for 0 and −5.00; กรอกทศนิยมได้ไม่เกิน 2 ตำแหน่ง for 18.005. An empty มีผลตั้งแต่วันที่ defaults to today rather than failing: the stored value equals CURDATE(), the field posts that same ISO date and the screen displays it as d MMM yyyy Thai short form with a Gregorian year — no 08/09/2026 appears anywhere, in any locale. Today is read from CURDATE(), never from a pinned clock (§2.6). FR-07, FR-48 · D-17, D-16, D-03
TC-AUTH-054 A rate carrying satang is legal and flows through to an amount unitP2 Rate fixture water 7.50
  1. Save water 7.50
  2. Compute the water item for 10 units
  3. Render the amount and the Thai words for a total carrying satang
 unit_price=7.50, quantity 10 · a total of 1790.50 for the words helper DECIMAL(8,2) accepts 7.50; the item is ROUND(10 × 7.50, 2) = 75.00. The words helper renders 1,790.50 as หนึ่งพันเจ็ดร้อยเก้าสิบบาทห้าสิบสตางค์ and 1,790.00 as หนึ่งพันเจ็ดร้อยเก้าสิบบาทถ้วน. No JavaScript multiplication is involved — the amount comes from SQL. FR-14, FR-24 · D-16, D-27

TC-AUTH-058 Approval freezes due_date = MAX(period due day, approval + 3 days) — the PRD §28 case unitP0 Pure function over (due_day, period, approval_date)
  1. Evaluate for the demo approval date
  2. Evaluate for a late approval
  3. Evaluate for a short month
 due_day=5, period 2026-09, approved 2026-09-01 · approved 2026-09-04 · due_day=28, period 2026-02, approved 2026-02-01 Case 1: MAX(2026-09-05, 2026-09-01 + 3 = 2026-09-04) = 2026-09-05, exactly the PRD §28 value. Case 2: MAX(2026-09-05, 2026-09-07) = 2026-09-07 — a shifted date is correct behaviour, not a defect. Case 3: 2026-02-28, and the month-end clamp never fires because the range is 1–28. FR-18 · D-20, D-44

The BILL message renders 📅 กรุณาชำระภายในวันที่ {that same date in full Thai month form, Gregorian year} — full month in LINE, short form on screen — with 💰 ยอดรวม 1,790 บาท (trailing .00 dropped in the message, kept as 1,790.00 in tables) and the closing line ชำระเงินสดที่สำนักงานนิติบุคคล เวลา จันทร์-ศุกร์ 09:00-17:00 น. — the seeded settings.office_hours verbatim, the same string TC-PAY-034 asserts byte-for-byte. TC-AUTH-061 Changing วันครบกำหนดชำระ never moves a bill that is already approved e2eP1 Bill 118 approved, its frozen due_date recorded as D₅ = expectedDueDate(PERIOD, 5); a second bill for room 206 is still Draft. No clock is pinned

  3. Approve the Draft bill the same day
 due_day: 5 → 10 · both oracles evaluated for today's CURDATE() Toast บันทึกการตั้งค่าแล้ว. Bill 118's due_date is still D₅ — frozen at approval, not recomputed on read. The newly approved bill gets expectedDueDate(PERIOD, 10), which on the PRD §28 calendar is MAX(2026-09-10, 2026-09-04) = 2026-09-10. Two bills in one period may therefore hold different due dates, and that is correct. FR-18 · D-20, D-22

4. Rooms, Tenants & Meter Readings

Sixty-nine executable test cases covering the master data every later screen reads and the reading round that turns it into money: Rooms (ห้องพัก), Tenants (ผู้เช่า) and Meter Reading (จดมิเตอร์). Everything downstream — the bill, the receipt, the LINE message — is a snapshot of a value entered on one of these three screens, so a defect here is silent and permanent: it does not crash, it charges the wrong number and prints it.

Each case is written to be run by one person against the seed.sql fixture of D-44 with a stopwatch and a browser, and every expected value is exact. Where a case is an API case the request is the endpoint from the engineering specification §3.2–§3.3 verbatim; where it is a UI case the copy is the Thai string from the UI design verbatim. A case that says "422 READING_BELOW_PREVIOUS" fails if the response is a 400, a 409, or a 422 carrying a different code.

Fixture detail specific to this section
Fixture
The D-44 seed of §1.3, unchanged.
Demo room
Room 205 = room id 41, floor 2, 30.00 ตร.ม.; tenant id 37; period 2026-09; readings 812 (water) / 813 (electric); bill 118, total 1,790.00; payment 96; receipt REC-202609-000852 (spec §3.1.10).
Sessions
"Admin" = a session for user id 1, "Staff" = a session for user id 2, "anonymous" = no session cookie at all.
Dates
Written on the documentation calendar (period 2026-09, today 6 ก.ย. 2026 — this document's own date). On a live run seed.sql computes every date relative to the run date (D-44) — reproduce the arithmetic, never the calendar.
Assumptions recorded by this section
  1. Room 205's tenant is สมชาย ใจดี · 0812345678, tenant id 37 — the engineering specification's fixture table (§3.1.10) and the db/seed.sql excerpt it publishes, which is the row a test actually meets. The UI design named room 205 นางสาวอารยา ศรีสุวรรณ and gave สมชาย ใจดี to room 202; it has since adopted the seed row and moved อารยา ศรีสุวรรณ to room 202, so the id (37), the room (41 / 205), the phone number and now the name agree across both documents. No case in this document asserts a literal tenant name as a pass condition — the assertion is that tenant_name_snapshot is stable across a later edit — so every case passed under either reading. The S3 documentation defect is closed.
  2. The binding floor range is 1–99 (spec §3.3.3). The room form's validation table says 1–127, which is the raw TINYINT range, not a product rule. TC-ROOM-007 asserts 1–99 and treats 100 as rejected.
  3. New rooms created by a test use codes outside the seed's 101–516 block — 517 and 518. The UI design's example toast "เพิ่มห้อง 209 แล้ว" uses a code the seed already occupies; it is an illustration, not a fixture.
  4. An out-of-range room area is rejected at the same boundary in the form and in the API (> 0 and ≤ 9999.99). The specification gives the range; the form gives the Thai sentence. Neither states which layer rejects first, so both are asserted.
  5. Room deactivation is reversible via เปิดใช้งานอีกครั้ง, per the room detail's success banner — but the engineering specification's endpoint catalogue §3.2.3 has POST /rooms/:id/deactivate and no counterpart, and §3.2 states that everything not listed does not exist. That was contradiction 11 in §9.3 and is now closed: the specification has added POST /rooms/:id/activate (Admin, _csrf only, audited ROOM_ACTIVATE, 303 back to the room, and a deliberate no-op rather than a 409 when the room is already active). TC-ROOM-018 keeps asserting the banner and the exclusion behaviour; the route itself is TC-ROOM-070–072.
stateDiagram-v2
  [*] --> NoBill: room has a tenant, no bill yet
  NoBill --> DraftBill: generation run, D-18
  DraftBill --> DraftBill: reading edited, bill recalculated in place
  DraftBill --> Approved: Approve and Send, D-19
  Approved --> DraftBill: Admin revert with reason, D-22
  NoBill: editable by Staff and Admin
  DraftBill: editable by Staff and Admin
  Approved: frozen for everyone
A reading has no status of its own. Its editability is derived from its room's bill for that period — the single rule TC-ROOM-063 to TC-ROOM-066 exercise from four directions (D-11, D-12, D-22, ADR §3.4).

4.1 Rooms — create, edit and field validation

Eleven cases on the Rooms form. Five of them are P0 because the two values they guard multiply into money on every future bill: the area (× 35.00 THB/ตร.ม. every month, forever) and the opening baselines (entered wrong, the room's first bill charges 1,260 units instead of 10).

IDTitleTypePriPreconditionsStepsTest dataExpected resultTraces to
TC-ROOM-001 Create a room with its opening meter baselines e2eP0 Admin session; no room with code 517 exists
  1. Open /rooms
  2. Press เพิ่มห้อง
  3. Fill every field
  4. Press บันทึก
 หมายเลขห้อง 517 · ชั้น 5 · ประเภทห้อง สตูดิโอ · ขนาดพื้นที่ 30.00 · เลขมิเตอร์น้ำเริ่มต้น 1250 · เลขมิเตอร์ไฟเริ่มต้น 5280 · วันที่เริ่มต้น 2026-08-31 303 → /rooms/:id for the new room. Toast เพิ่มห้อง 517 แล้ว. Detail shows ขนาดพื้นที่ 30.00 ตร.ม., สถานะ ว่าง with the hint กำหนดจากผู้เช่าโดยอัตโนมัติ, and the three baseline fields still editable. One audit_log row, action ROOM_CREATE, actor_user_id = 1. Chip counts become ทั้งหมด 81 · มีผู้เช่า 67 · ว่าง 14 · ปิดใช้งาน 0. FR-04, FR-41, D-47, D-06, D-07

Through the form: 200, the page re-renders (no redirect) with บันทึกไม่สำเร็จ — ตรวจสอบ 1 ช่องด้านล่าง, the field error หมายเลขห้องนี้ถูกใช้แล้ว กรุณาใช้หมายเลขอื่น, and every typed value still present. TC-ROOM-004 Room area boundaries — the number every common-area fee multiplies APIP0 Admin session

  2. Read back the stored value for the accepted ones

On submit: no redirect, no full-page reload, บันทึกไม่สำเร็จ — ตรวจสอบ 1 ช่องด้านล่าง above the form and ข้อมูลที่กรอกไว้ยังอยู่ครบ แก้ไขแล้วกด บันทึก อีกครั้ง; the exact characters typed are still in the input.
